# Kazakhstan — English books

**KAZAKHSTAN OPENED 2026-11-03 under the user's
all-closed-countries ruling.** Kazakhstan is CLOSED in the
library. Its English set as recorded is 34 files — Grades
1–10, all student-side, **no teacher books at all** — and all
34 are in hand, read whole, and filed below (4,923 PDF pages,
675 citation anchors, all passed). Two pairs of files are the
same book twice, so the set holds **32 distinct books**: the
Grade 7 English Plus file is held byte-identically under two
record rows (#1626 = #287), and the Grade 4 Smiles book is
held as an interior-only print file (#114) and again with its
covers (#752). There are no link-only English books.

Kazakhstan does not run one national English course: the
ministry's approved list (Order №216) carries several
competing commercial series per grade, and the record holds
them side by side. Primary (G1–G4) runs *Smiles* and
*English Language for Kazakhstan* (Express Publishing),
*Top Stars* (MM Publications) and Macmillan's *English for
Kazakhstan* (recorded as "Give me five!"). Grades 5–9 run
four series in parallel — *Excel* (Express), *English Plus*
(Oxford), *Full Blast* (MM) and *Laser* (Macmillan) — with
English Plus the only line that also brings its workbooks.
Grade 10 holds a single bespoke science-stream book by the
Kazakh publisher «Study Inn». **No Grade 11–12 English books
exist in the record at all.**

**Grab state 2026-11-03:** reading copies of Grades 1–2 were
already in staging; the other 32 files were pulled from the
secured GitHub library through the pusher's credential route
(repo tree + mid-size-books / full-quality-oversized-books
releases), 970 MB in total. Every file's PDF page count
matches the record exactly (34/34), and every book's identity
was checked from its own cover/title/imprint — which produced
the corrections below. Many copies are scans or the
publishers' own print files rather than born-digital PDFs;
each section states its copy's character and defects.

    corrected by the full read filed below.

**Series verdicts (what the set does and does not teach):**
the primary ceiling is low — in the Grade 4 Express book the
whole past tense is *was/were* (no lexical past forms, no
*did* questions), and no primary book teaches future forms,
present perfect, passive, conditionals or reported speech. By
Grade 9 all four secondary series teach the passive and Type
3 conditionals, and present perfect continuous arrives from
Grade 6 — but **reported speech is a split**: English Plus
and Excel teach it, Laser B1+ never does (its own filing
names the gap), and Full Blast ends its subordination there.
No book in the set teaches mixed or inverted conditionals;
English Plus's passive stops at the simple forms (no
causative, no impersonal passive). Pronunciation is a garnish
in every series — a box per module at best, and Full Blast
Grade 9 has no pronunciation teaching in its modules at all.
Kazakhstan content is real but bounded: a "My Country" or
Culture page per unit in most series, trilingual word lists
in the Express and MM books, and Abai's *Book of Words*
inside the Grade 9 English Plus set. Grade 10's lone
science book's grammar teaching is thinner than its own
reference section (several units present no new grammar; the
reference teaches structures no unit practises).

**Still owed by the library side:** the real Smiles 2
Pupil's Book (correction 2); the English Plus Grade 7 Second
Edition the record claims (correction 1); a complete copy of
Full Blast Grade 6 (correction 12); a proper scan of Full
Blast Grade 9 (correction 13); any Grade 11–12 English books
(none in the record); and teacher books for every title
(none in the record).

## Grade 1 — Smiles 1 Pupil's Book for Kazakhstan Grade 1

### Identity block

- **Real title as printed (front cover, PDF p. 1; title page, PDF p. 6):** *Smiles 1 — Pupil's Book for Kazakhstan Grade 1*
- **Authors as printed (cover and title page):** Jenny Dooley – Virginia Evans (the record lists "Evans & Dooley"; the book prints Dooley first)
- **Publisher:** Express Publishing
- **Edition / year as printed:** The Kazakh imprint line on the title page (PDF p. 6) reads «Экспресс Паблишин баспасы 2016» — printed year **2016**, matching the record. No other edition statement is printed.
- **ISBN as printed (back cover, PDF p. 110):** ISBN 978-1-4715-4507-8
- **PDF pages:** 110
- **Printed page numbers / offset:** The PDF opens with unnumbered apparatus: PDF p. 1 = front cover, PDF pp. 2–3 = sticker pages (Modules 1–6), PDF pp. 4–5 = cut-out pages, PDF p. 6 = title page, PDF p. 7 = Contents. The numbered book then runs printed p. 3 (PDF p. 8, "Let's Start!") to printed p. 104 (PDF p. 109, last Pictionary page), so **printed page = PDF page − 5** throughout the numbered book. PDF p. 110 = back cover. All page references below are to the **printed** pages.
- **Identity verdict:** The file matches the record — Smiles 1 for Kazakhstan, Grade 1 pupil's book, Express Publishing, 2016. Only the author order differs from the record (book prints Jenny Dooley – Virginia Evans).
- **Text layer:** Unusable — the PDF's only embedded text is the okulyk.kz watermark overlay repeated on every page; the book itself is page images. The book was read from page renders (extracted page images viewed directly, plus OCR of those renders as a search index). Citation anchors below were verified against the OCR/render of the exact page cited.
- **How the book is built:** A "Let's Start!" opener (p. 3), six 14-page modules, each built the same way: a photo/cartoon opener presenting the vocabulary with a song, a four-to-five-frame cartoon story ("Listen and read") with the course characters (Lilly, her brother Liam, Charlie the monkey and Nanny), picture practice, a values/cross-curricular spread and a project, closing with a "Check Point" self-check spread with a "My Smiles Report" smiley self-rating. Then "All About My World" (pp. 88–98), ten short photo topics, and a trilingual (English–Kazakh–Russian) Pictionary (pp. 99–104). Sticker and cut-out pages are bound at the front of this PDF.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Let's Start! (p. 3).** First greetings: the cartoon cast say hello and goodbye by name, the class sings the Smile song, and pupils colour the smiling-face mascot. Formulaic only: *Hello! / Goodbye!*

**Module 1 — My Family (pp. 4–17).** Family words (Mummy, Daddy, brother, sister) introduced with photos of Kazakh children and their families (pp. 4–5), then a cartoon in which Lilly presents her family: *Hello! I'm Lilly. This is my mummy.* Names and introductions are practised through *What's your name? — I'm …*. The second half teaches the colours red, blue, green, yellow, purple, orange through a colour song and games: *What colour is it?* A "My favourite colour" spread has pupils poll the class, and the module story is "The Queen Bee", billed as a story from Germany, in which the colours are chanted to call the bees home. Check Point 1 (pp. 16–17) recycles the module with sticker faces, listen-and-circle and a camel colour-pattern puzzle.

**Module 2 — My School (pp. 18–31).** Numbers first: 1–5 on the opener (p. 18) with the Numbers song and a pair game ("One. What colour is it?"), a listening cartoon about a school-bus scramble (p. 19), then 6–10 with *How old are you?* School things follow (schoolbag, book, pen, pencil, rubber): *It's a blue schoolbag*, counting classroom objects (*How many pens? — Six pens*), and a story in which the cast's school things get mixed up. The module closes with practice and a Check Point.

**Module 3 — My Room (pp. 32–45).** Furniture (bed, chair, TV, table, desk) presented by colouring a room by number (p. 32, model sentence *This is my bed. It's …*) and a listening cartoon in which a boy proudly shows his room and jumps on the TV (p. 33). Toys come next (doll, kite, bike, car, plane, train) with *I've got a kite*, position work around the furniture (*Look at the bed* — things on/under it), and the action imperatives *stand up, sit down, clap your hands, stamp your feet, jump, dance* in a song-and-do spread. Project: "My Favourite Toys!"

**Module 4 — My Pets (pp. 46–59).** Pets (rabbit, fish, cat, dog, mouse, tortoise, parrot, spider) introduced by completing the words (p. 46, model *This is my pet. It's a cat*) and a pet-shop cartoon (p. 47) in which Meg presents her fish Fin and a parrot named Charlie causes chaos; the language is *This is my pet. It's a …* and *What's its name?* Further spreads add the colours black, brown, white, pink and the size pair big/small (*Is it big?*), pets in odd places (*There's a tortoise on the table*), and a pet-shop role play built on *I've got a spider in my pet shop*. Project: "My Pet!"

**Module 5 — My Food (pp. 60–73).** Food and drink (milk, cheese, bananas, juice, eggs, chicken, apples, carrots, pears, onions, tomatoes) unscrambled on the opener (p. 60), then a farm cartoon (p. 61): a cow offers milk, cheese and eggs are offered in turn — *I've got some milk for you! — I like milk. Yum!* — ending with an egg cracking on Liam's head: *Oh, no! I don't like eggs. Yuk!* Later spreads practise *I like / I don't like*, asking politely (*Can I have some …? Give me some, please*), saying what is in a basket, and the feelings *I'm hungry / I'm thirsty*. Project: "My Favourite Food!"

**Module 6 — My Music (pp. 74–87).** Instruments (trumpet, guitar, drum, piano, recorder) on the opener (p. 74, model *I can play the drum. Listen!*) and a cartoon (p. 75) in which Lilly's trumpet and Liam's guitar are unwelcome at home ("No, Lilly! Not now!") until Nanny applauds the garden concert: *Music is fun!* The second strand is the face (hair, eyes, nose, ears, mouth): *Touch your ears!* and describing a funny face — *I've got green hair and blue eyes*. The verbs sing and skip ride along in the songs. Project: "My Musical Instrument!"

**All About My World (pp. 88–98).** Ten one-page photo topics recycling the book's language on real-world content: "Welcome to My Kazakhstan!" (pp. 88–89) — the flag ("It is blue and yellow"), Astana as the capital, the President named as Nursultan Nazarbayev (as printed, 2016), the country's rivers, mountains and lakes, and Gorki Park in Almaty; then My Friend, My Friend's Family, Shapes, Clothes, Cutlery, In the Zoo, Injuries, Travelling and Favourite Places.

**Pictionary (pp. 99–104).** A picture dictionary of the book's whole vocabulary, every entry glossed in Kazakh and Russian (e.g. p. 104: piano, recorder, sing, skip, hair, eyes, nose, ears, mouth).

**Grammar spine:** To be (I'm …, It's a …) in statements and the fixed questions What's your name? / What colour is it? / How many …? / How old are you? / Is it big?; *this is my …*; *I've got …* for possession (including *I've got some … for you*); *I like / I don't like* + food noun; *can* for ability (*I can play the drum*); imperatives for classroom actions and *Touch your …!*; *there's a … on the …* in the pets module. Everything is formulaic and oral-first; no paradigm is ever tabulated.

### Honest negatives — what this book does not teach

- **No past or future forms, no present continuous as a structure, no comparatives, no possessives beyond *my/your/its*, no question system** beyond the memorised frames listed above.
- **No alphabet or phonics strand.** Letters appear only inside word-completion and unscramble tasks (f_s_, c_t, e-e-c-e-h-s); the letter names and sounds are never taught in this book.
- **No numbers beyond 10, no days, months, seasons, weather, time or school subjects.**
- **Reading is minimal**: speech-bubble cartoons, captions and labels only; there is no continuous prose text except the one-page "My World" photo captions. Writing is word-level copying and completion.
- The Kazakhstan page's political content is dated as printed (President Nursultan Nazarbayev, capital called Astana) — recorded as printed, not updated.

## Grade 2 — Smiles 2 for Kazakhstan — ⚠ IDENTITY MISMATCH: this file is the ACTIVITY BOOK inside Pupil's Book covers, and it is incomplete

Record title it was filed under: "Smiles 2 for Kazakhstan Pupil's Book", Evans & Dooley, Express Publishing, 2017, 95 PDF pages. **Verdict: MISMATCH in kind.** The PDF's two outer covers belong to the Pupil's Book, but every interior page belongs to the companion **Activity Book** (the workbook), and the Activity Book's own final section is missing. What follows is the verified identity of the file actually held and a summary of the workbook content it does contain. No Pupil's Book lesson content can be filed from this file.

### Identity block

- **Front cover (PDF p. 1):** *Smiles 2 — Pupil's Book for Kazakhstan Grade 2*, Jenny Dooley – Virginia Evans, Express Publishing. **Back cover (PDF p. 95)** is the Pupil's Book back cover, ISBN **978-1-4715-4826-0**, and PDF p. 94 is a combined front/back Pupil's Book cover spread carrying the same credits and ISBN.
- **The interior's own title page (PDF p. 2):** *Smiles 2 — Activity Book for Kazakhstan Grade 2*; author Jenny Dooley; Series Consultant: Bob Obee; Translations by N. Mukhamedjanova; Express Publishing; Kazakh imprint line «Экспресс Паблишин баспасы 2017» — printed year **2017** (matches the record's year, but for the Activity Book, not the Pupil's Book).
- **PDF pages:** 95. **Structure of the file:** PDF p. 1 = Pupil's Book front cover; PDF p. 2 = Activity Book title page; PDF p. 3 = Activity Book imprint/apparatus page; PDF p. 4 = Activity Book Contents (production slug "Page 3"); PDF pp. 5–93 = Activity Book printed pp. 4–92; PDF p. 94 = Pupil's Book cover spread; PDF p. 95 = Pupil's Book back cover.
- **Printed page numbers / offset:** **printed page = PDF page − 1** for the whole Activity Book interior (e.g. Unit 1 opens on printed p. 4 = PDF p. 5; My Green Passport ends on printed p. 92 = PDF p. 93). Every interior page also carries its Quark production slug (e.g. "02 Smiles 2 KAZAKH Mod 1 Act NEW.qxp … Page 4", file dates 1/23/17–5/10/17) and crop marks — this is the print file, not a scan of a finished copy. All page references below are to the **printed** (Activity Book) pages.
- **What is missing:** The Contents (printed p. 3) promises **Story Cutouts (pp. 93–126)** after My Green Passport; the file stops at printed p. 92 and closes with the Pupil's Book covers instead. The 34 cut-out pages are absent. The sticker sheets the exercises refer to ("find the Stickers at the back of the book") are likewise not in this file.
- **How the workbook is built:** Eight 8-page units shadowing the Pupil's Book unit by unit — every page is footed "Cover this page after page N of the Pupil's Book" — then a one-page Round-up (p. 68), a "Sing & Do!" song section (pp. 69–89) and the "My Green Passport" environmental section (pp. 90–92). All tasks are written/picture work: complete, match, tick, colour, draw and write, wordsearches, sticker tasks and board games. The Pupil's Book's own syllabus (from the workbook's Contents topics) runs: about me/commands/shapes/colours; family, house and friends; school; the world around us (house, pets); health and body; traditions (numbers 1–50, food, birthdays); the natural environment (animals, actions, seasons, weather); holidays and travel (clothes, weather, seasons).

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Unit 1 — All About Me (pp. 4–11).** Opens with a personal form to complete and draw (name, height, hair colour, eye colour; "My Family" and "Things I Like" boxes, p. 4), then classroom commands completed letter by letter and matched to Charlie the monkey miming them — *Stand up! Sit down! Open your book! Close your book!* (p. 4). Shapes are ticked inside composite pictures (rectangle, square, circle, triangle, p. 5); colours are written under photos (red apple given; blue balloon, pink flamingo, p. 6); a sticker page sorts the child's world into balloons labelled Me / My Family / My favourite colour / My favourite thing / My favourite subject / I like wearing / I can play (p. 7). The unit's Storytime page (p. 8) is a colouring picture of the rainbow painter with two lines to complete ("Look at all the c_l__r_!", "Well done, little __rd_!"). A spiral board game closes the unit (p. 11).

**Unit 2 — My Family and Friends (pp. 12–19).** Family presentation sentences are matched to framed portraits (p. 12): *This is my mummy and daddy. This is my grandma and grandpa. This is my sister Lilly and me. This is Charlie.* The remaining pages practise the family set, things in a house and friends in the same complete/match/colour formats, each keyed to a Pupil's Book spread.

**Unit 3 — My School (pp. 20–27).** School things are found in a maze-like matching task and written as full sentences (p. 20): *What's this? — It's a schoolbag* (pen, pencil, book, ruler, rubber). Around-school places and class-photo tasks fill the rest of the unit.

**Unit 4 — The World Around Us (pp. 28–35).** Rooms and being in them, practised as yes/no questions with short answers (p. 28): *Is Lilly in the garden? — Yes, she is.* / *Is Liam in the kitchen? — No, he isn't* (he is asleep in bed; Charlie, asked about the tree house, is in the bath). Pets, my things and "Where is it from?" complete the unit's topics per the Contents.

**Unit 5 — Health and Body (pp. 36–43).** Body words (hair, eyes, mouth, ears, nose) are found in a wordsearch, matched to pictures and written (p. 36); the same page's reading task describes a clown — *He's got yellow hair. He's got green eyes…* — to be coloured from the text, i.e. *have got* for appearance. Size and health topics follow per the Contents.

**Unit 6 — Traditions and Customs (pp. 44–51).** Numbers and age through birthday cakes (p. 44): each child holds a sum (five + five; twelve − four) and the pupil counts the candles, works out the age and completes *I'm ten.* The unit's other strands are numbers 1–50, food, birthdays and "What's it for?" per the Contents.

**Unit 7 — The Natural Environment (pp. 52–59).** Ability with animal comparisons (p. 52): *I can jump like a frog! I can swim like a fish! I can dance like a chimp! I can sing like a bird!* — matched to the right animal pictures. Animals, actions, seasons and weather fill the unit.

**Unit 8 — My Holidays and Travel (pp. 60–67).** Clothes with colours as full noun phrases (p. 60): colour the right picture for *red shorts, a blue T-shirt, a yellow dress, a green skirt, a blue hat, pink jeans*. Weather, seasons and holiday/travel tasks complete the unit.

**Round-up (p. 68).** A category-sorting page that inventories the whole course's topic sets in pairs: shapes/colours, school things/around the school, rooms/family members, toys/houses, body/food, days/numbers, animals/seasons, clothes/weather.

**Sing & Do! (pp. 69–89).** The workbook's song section (divider p. 69): the Pupil's Book songs with actions, in write-and-do format.

**My Green Passport (pp. 90–92).** Environmental tasks: wooden toys praised as "great and friendly to the planet" over a join-the-dots Pinocchio (p. 91), a "No rubbish!" colour-by-code page, "Make new things from old" — what can be made from an old can (flower pot, money box, pencil pot) — and a "Clean Air Day Every Day!" poster to colour: *Ride a bike or walk* (p. 92).

**Grammar spine (as exercised in the workbook):** *this is my …*; *it's a …* with *What's this?*; *be* in place questions with short answers (*Is she in the garden? — Yes, she is / No, she isn't*); *have got* for appearance (*He's got yellow hair*); *can* for ability (*I can jump like a frog*); numbers to 50 and age with *I'm …*; colour + clothes phrases with *a*. The workbook only ever practises these in one-to-three-line written tasks; the presentation of every point lives in the missing Pupil's Book.

## Grade 3 — English Language for Kazakhstan Grade 3, Pupil's Book

### Identity block

- **Real title as printed (front cover, PDF p. 1; title page, PDF p. 3):** *English Language for Kazakhstan Grade 3* — cover adds "with DIGI APP"; the file is headed "Digital Flip Book" on the cover.
- **Authors as printed (cover and title page):** Jenny Dooley – Bob Obee; **Series Consultant: Natalya Mukhamedjanova** (the record lists Mukhamedjanova as a third author; the book credits her as series consultant). Illustrations by Assel Nurmanbetova (title page).
- **Publisher:** Express Publishing. The imprint (PDF p. 2) thanks LLP "EDU Stream" for the photo shooting; the record's "EDU Stream + Express Publishing" is therefore broadly right, but the publishing imprint printed in the book is Express Publishing.
- **Edition / year as printed:** "First published 2024" (imprint, PDF p. 2); © Jenny Dooley, Bob Obee 2024. Matches the record's 2024.
- **ISBN as printed (back cover, PDF p. 116):** ISBN 978-1-3992-1415-5 (the ISBN-13 check digit computes to 5, so the back cover is the valid form).
- **PDF pages:** 116
- **Printed page numbers / offset:** **printed page = PDF page − 2** throughout (imprint = PDF p. 2; Contents on printed pp. 4–5 = PDF pp. 6–7; Module 1 opens on printed p. 6 = PDF p. 8; Glossary ends on printed p. 112 = PDF p. 114; PDF p. 115 is an unnumbered Alphabet poster page and PDF p. 116 the back cover). All page references below are to the **printed** pages.
- **Identity verdict:** File matches the record (Grade 3, Express Publishing, 2024), with the two credit nuances above: Mukhamedjanova is series consultant, not co-author, and EDU Stream is thanked for photography rather than named as co-publisher.
- **Text layer:** Unusable — the PDF's only embedded text is the okulyk watermark overlay; the book is page images, read from page renders with OCR of the renders as a search index. Citation anchors were verified against the OCR/render of the exact page cited.
- **How the book is built:** Eight modules of 12 pages, each split into two six-page sub-units, with a phonics/sight-words strand ("Sounds and Words"), a CLIL page and a named writing task per module (set out in the Contents syllabus tables, printed pp. 4–5). After Modules 4 and 8 come "A World of Wonder" spreads (pp. 54–55, 104–105); two Storytime fables with stated values close the course (pp. 106–107), then a trilingual Glossary with IPA transcriptions (pp. 108–112) and an Alphabet poster (PDF p. 115). A robot mascot, ROLO, fronts the digital material.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Module 1 — Hello, English! (pp. 6–17).** Greetings and names, and About Me. Vocabulary: the colours (black, blue, green, orange, purple, red, white, yellow) and numbers 1–10. Grammar (per the Contents and the module's presentations): the verb *to be* and personal subject pronouns. Sounds and Words: the English alphabet a–h. CLIL (Art): primary and secondary colours. Reading: "Meeting ROLO"; writing: an All About Me page.

**Module 2 — My School (pp. 18–29).** My schoolbag and classroom activities. Vocabulary: school things (book, desk, notebook, pen, pencil, pencil case, rubber, ruler, schoolbag, sharpener), numbers 11–20, size/age adjectives (big, long, new, old, short, small) and classroom instructions (be quiet, close your book, open your schoolbag, point to the door, put your hand up, sit down, stand up, take out your book). Grammar: plurals in *-s*, *to be*, *this/these – that/those*, *there is – there are*, prepositions of place and the imperative. Alphabet i–q. CLIL (PE): classroom activities. Reading: "First day back at school"; writing: "A Schoolbag full of Colours!"

**Module 3 — People I Love (pp. 30–41).** My family and my friends. Vocabulary: family (brother, dad, grandma, grandpa, mum, sister), character adjectives (clever, friendly, funny, kind, naughty, shy) and appearance (beard, brown eyes, curly hair, dark hair, fair hair, glasses, moustache, ponytail). Grammar: possessive adjectives, *have got*, *to be*, subject and object pronouns, and the present simple (its first appearance). Alphabet r–z completes the alphabet strand. CLIL (Social Science): feelings. Reading: "Helping mummy cat and her babies"; writing: My Family Tree.

**Module 4 — Weather (pp. 42–53).** Seasons and weather, and clothes I wear. Vocabulary: the four seasons; weather phrases (*It's cold. It's hot. It's rainy. It's snowy. It's sunny. It's windy.*); clothes (boots, dress, gloves, hat, jacket, jeans, shoes, shorts, skirt, T-shirt). Grammar: *a – an – the*, the present simple, personal pronouns, prepositions of time and adjectives. Sounds and Words: the letters e, r, d, p, n (red, pen) and sight words *it, is, a, and, isn't*. CLIL (Science): a bear's life in different seasons. Reading: "Flying kites"; writing: My Favourite Season! **A World of Wonder 1 (pp. 54–55)** then recycles Modules 1–4.

**Module 5 — My Free Time (pp. 56–67).** "I can do this!" and toys and sport. Vocabulary: action verbs (climb, cook, dance, draw, jump, run, sing, skateboard), toys (ball, car, jigsaw puzzle, robot, scooter, teddy bear, toy bricks, train) and free-time activities (go to the park, play football, play tennis, play the guitar, play volleyball, ride a bike). Grammar: the verb *can*, *a – an – the* again, *like + verb-ing*, the present simple, the imperative and *let's + verb*. Sounds: a, h, t, m, u (hat, mum, dad); sight words *I, my, you, and, can't, are*. CLIL (Geography): safety in the playground. Reading: "Playing with toys"; writing: My Toy Design.

**Module 6 — Health (pp. 68–79).** Body parts and healthy food. Vocabulary: body-action phrases (bend your knees, clap your hands, hop on one leg, stamp your feet, stretch your arms, touch your head, plus ears, eyes, mouth, nose) and food and drinks (apple, banana, broccoli, cake, carrot, cheese, chicken, chocolate, ice cream, juice, onion, orange, pear, popcorn, potato, rice, water). Grammar: plurals, subject and object pronouns, *have got*, the present simple, *some – any – a – an* and *there is – there are*. Sounds: i, b, s, g, x (big, six); sight words *there, small, is, and, a, it*. CLIL (Science): food that is good/bad for our teeth. Reading: "A smoothie shower"; writing: My 5 A Day Poster.

**Module 7 — Buildings (pp. 80–91).** My home, rooms and objects; buildings in our town. Vocabulary: parts of a house (bathroom, bedroom, garden, hall, kitchen, living room; armchair, bath, bed, bookcase, chair, clock, cooker, cupboard, lamp, mat, mirror, sofa, table, TV, wardrobe) and numbers 21–100 (the glossary lists thirty, forty, fifty, sixty, seventy, eighty, ninety, a hundred). Grammar: plurals in *-es* and *-ies*, interrogative pronouns, *a – an – the*, *here – there*, *this/these*, *there is – there are* and prepositions of place. Sounds: j, l, v, y (jam, leg, van, yo-yo); sight words *we, what, do, some, any, here*. CLIL (Maths): odd and even numbers. Reading: "Uncle Lucas's cool caravan"; writing: My Favourite Street.

**Module 8 — My Holidays (pp. 92–103).** Family holidays and transport. Vocabulary: holiday things (sandals, suitcase, sunbathe, suncream, sunglasses, swimming trunks, swimsuit, towel), transport (boat, bus, car, helicopter, lorry, motorbike, plane, train) and places (cinema, funfair, beach, café, swimming pool, zoo). Grammar: adjectives, question words, *here – there*, prepositions of place and time and *can*. Sounds: k, q, z (quilt, koala, zip); sight words *find, come, help, go, who, with*. CLIL (Science): what needs water? Reading: "Holidays"; writing: a postcard, and "Be Safe at the Beach!" **A World of Wonder 2 (pp. 104–105)** recycles Modules 5–8 (doll's house, hot-air balloon, ride).

**Storytime (pp. 106–107).** Two illustrated fables told in short past-tense narration: "The Ant and the Cricket" (value: Always work hard!) and the Kazakh tale "Aldar Kosse and the Greedy Farmer" (value: Be clever. Think before you act!) — the glossary singles out their story vocabulary (ant, cricket, hungry, lazy; clever, fall down, king, greedy, silly, sky, trick, wheat), the book's only sustained past-tense reading.

**Glossary (pp. 108–112).** Every module's wordlist in English with IPA transcription and Kazakh and Russian equivalents, closing with the two stories' wordlists.

**Grammar spine:** The verb *to be* and subject/object pronouns; plurals (*-s*, then *-es/-ies*); *this/these – that/those*; *there is/are*; prepositions of place and time; the imperative and *let's*; possessive adjectives; *have got*; the present simple (from Module 3, never contrasted with any other tense); *a – an – the*; *some/any*; *can* for ability; *like + -ing*; question words and interrogative pronouns. The past simple exists only inside the two closing fables' narration and is never presented as a grammar point.

### Honest negatives — what this book does not teach

- **No taught past tenses and no future forms**: *was/were*, the past simple and *will/going to* are never presented; past forms occur only untaught inside the Storytime fables.
- **No present continuous, no present perfect, no comparatives or superlatives, no possessive *'s*** (possessive adjectives only), **no *must/should/have to***.
- **No telling the time, no days of the week or months** in the syllabus (seasons only), **no weather questions system** beyond the *It's …* phrases, and numbers stop at 100.
- Writing stays at caption, poster, postcard and family-tree level; there is no paragraph writing.

## Grade 3 — Top Stars for Kazakhstan Grade 3 Student's Book

### Identity block

- **Real title as printed (front cover, PDF p. 1; title page, PDF p. 3):** *Top Stars for Kazakhstan — Student's Book 3*
- **Authors as printed (cover, title page and imprint):** H. Q. Mitchell – Marileni Malkogianni – Konstantina Vasileiou (record: "Mitchell/Malkogianni/Vasileiou" ✓)
- **Publisher:** MM Publications ("Produced in the EU", imprint)
- **Edition / year as printed:** Copyright © 2024 MM Publications (imprint, PDF p. 115 — in this flip file the imprint page sits after the Wordlist, before the back cover). Matches the record's 2024. No other edition statement.
- **ISBN as printed (imprint, PDF p. 115, and back cover, PDF p. 116):** ISBN 978-618-05-7217-9
- **PDF pages:** 116
- **Printed page numbers / offset:** **printed page = PDF page − 2** for all foliated pages (Module 1, Lesson 1 "Alphabet" is printed p. 6 = PDF p. 8; the Wordlist runs printed pp. 105–112 = PDF pp. 107–114). Front matter: PDF p. 1 cover, p. 2 imprint/copyright leaf, p. 3 title page, p. 4 welcome letter, pp. 5–7 Contents (printed pp. 4–5 plus continuation). PDF p. 115 = imprint page repeated at the back; PDF p. 116 = back cover. All page references below are to the **printed** pages.
- **Identity verdict:** The file matches the record exactly (Grade 3, MM Publications, 2024).
- **Text layer:** Effectively unusable — apart from a few cover/imprint fragments, the PDF's embedded text is the okulyk watermark overlay; the book is page images (many stored as JPEG 2000), read from page renders with OCR of the renders as a search index. Citation anchors were verified against the OCR/render of the exact page cited.
- **How the book is built:** Eight 12-page modules, each with three lessons, a phonics slot and a CLIL/cross-curricular slot (all mapped in the Contents tables, printed pp. 4–5). After every two modules there is a two-page illustrated Story with a stated value (Stories 1–4, pp. 30, 54, 78, 102); Culture pages on Kazakhstan themes sit inside Modules 1, 3, 5 and 7 (pp. 18, 42, 66, 90); an Earth Day spread (p. 104) and a trilingual Wordlist with phonetic transcriptions (pp. 105–112) close the book. A green monster mascot fronts the course.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Module 1 — Hello English! (pp. 6–17).** Greetings and names; About me. Lesson 1 is the alphabet itself (printed p. 6): the letters A–L with their letter-name transcriptions, circling and matching upper- and lower-case forms; the alphabet is completed across the module's phonics slots, which then drill short *a* /æ/ (cat, hat, van). Grammar: personal subject pronouns, the verb *be* (affirmative), and the exchanges *What's your name? — I'm … / My name is …; How are you? — Fine, thanks; How old are you?; Where are you from?* Vocabulary: greetings, imperatives, numbers 1–10, countries and colours. CLIL (Geography, Art): flags. **Culture Page 1 (p. 18): An Interview.**

**Module 2 — My School (pp. 20–29).** My schoolbag, my classroom and classroom activities. Grammar: imperatives, the article *a/an*, *have got* (affirmative/negative), *What's this/that?*, *this/that/these/those*, *there's … / there are …*, prepositions of place (*on, in, under*) and plurals in *-s/-es*. Vocabulary: classroom language and objects, items in a house, numbers 11–20. Phonics: the alphabet with short *e* /e/ (pen, desk, chest). CLIL (Maths): calculations. **Story 1 (p. 30, Modules 1–2)** — value: "Learn and play every day!"

**Module 3 — People I Love (pp. 32–41).** My family; my friends. Grammar: the verb *be* in negative, questions and short answers; *Who's that? — It's …*; *have got* in all forms; *there isn't/aren't …*; object pronouns (*me, it*); telling the time — *What's the time? It's …* (o'clock); the present simple affirmative (third-person *-s*); prepositions of time (*on, at*). Vocabulary: family members, adjectives, physical appearance, actions and the days of the week. Phonics: voiced *th* /ð/ (brother, that) and unvoiced *th* /θ/ (three, Thelma). CLIL (Social Studies): my family. **Culture Page 2 (p. 42): Famous people from Kazakhstan.**

**Module 4 — Weather (pp. 44–53).** Seasons and weather; clothes I wear. Grammar: the present simple in affirmative, negative, questions and short answers; prepositions of time (*in*); object pronouns (*him, her*); the articles (*a/an, the*); the polite pattern *Give me (my coat), please.* Vocabulary: seasons, actions, weather and clothes. Phonics: *ir* /ɜː/ (shirt, skirt) and *or* /ɔː/ (short). CLIL (Social Studies): traditional costumes. **Story 2 (p. 54, Modules 3–4)** — value: "Spend time with your friends."

**Module 5 — My Free Time (pp. 56–65).** "I can do this!" and toys and sports ("I like …"). Grammar: *can* for ability (affirmative/negative), *let's …*, *go + verb-ing*, *like + verb-ing*, the present simple negative and the articles. Vocabulary: actions, animals, sports, toys and colours. Phonics: short *i* /ɪ/ (six, big, fish) and short *o* /ɒ/ (dolls, dog). CLIL (Social Studies): kite flying. **Culture Page 3 (p. 66): A traditional game from Kazakhstan.**

**Module 6 — Health (pp. 68–77).** Body parts; healthy food. Grammar: *have got* in questions and short answers, irregular plurals, the present simple in questions and short answers, *this/that/these/those* again, and *there is/are* in negative, questions and short answers. Vocabulary: parts of the face and body, food and drink. Phonics: *ch* /tʃ/ (cheese, chicken) and *sh* /ʃ/ (fish). CLIL (Health): My Plate. **Story 3 (p. 78, Modules 5–6)** — value: "Share moments with family and friends."

**Module 7 — Buildings (pp. 80–89).** My house, my room and objects; buildings in our town. Grammar: prepositions of place (*on, under, in, next to, between, behind*), adverbs of place (*here, there*), *there is/are* in all forms, the articles, *What's this/that? — This/That is …*, *What are these/those? — These/Those are …*, *have got*, and plurals in *-s, -es, -ves*. Vocabulary: items and rooms in a house, toys, numbers 20–100 and places in a town/city (the Wordlist adds museum, restaurant, amusement park, cinema, stadium, opera house, city, shop). Phonics: long *a* /eɪ/ (skateboard, table). CLIL (Geography): wonderful cities. **Culture Page 4 (p. 90): Day and Night in Astana.**

**Module 8 — My Holidays (pp. 92–101).** Family holidays; transport. Grammar: the question words *who / where / when / what*; prepositions of place (*in front of, opposite, near*); *can* in questions and short answers; prepositions of time (*in, on, at*); *How do you get there? — By …*; *How much is/are …?* Vocabulary: geographical features (island, village), means of transport (plane, bus, train, ship in the Wordlist), holiday actions (*go hiking, go fishing, go mountain climbing, feed the animals, take photos*), souvenirs and prices, with money words (tenge, dollar, pound) in the CLIL slot. Phonics: short *o* and long *o* /əʊ/ (photo). CLIL (Maths): different currencies. **Story 4 (p. 102, Modules 7–8)** — value: "Forgive other people." **Earth Day (p. 104)** recycles the environmental lexis (help, Earth, recycle, turn off, water).

**Wordlist (pp. 105–112).** The course vocabulary by module and section, each entry with part of speech, phonetic transcription and Kazakh and Russian equivalents.

**Grammar spine:** The verb *be* in all forms; *have got* in all forms; *there is/are* in all forms; *this/that/these/those*; plurals (*-s, -es, -ves*, plus irregular plurals in Module 6); articles *a/an/the*; prepositions of place and time; the present simple built up across Modules 3–6 (affirmative with *-s*, then negative, then questions and short answers — always as a routine/fact tense, with no time-line contrast); *can* for ability; *like + -ing* and *go + -ing*; question words *who/where/when/what*; *how much is/are …?* and telling the time with o'clock. A systematic phonics strand runs through every module (alphabet and short vowels, *th*, *ir/or*, *ch/sh*, long *a* and *o*).

### Honest negatives — what this book does not teach

- **No past tenses and no future forms are taught**: there is no past simple, no *was/were* presentation and no *will/going to* anywhere in the Contents grammar map; the four Stories are the only narrative texts and their language is not presented as a grammar point.
- **No present continuous** (the *-ing* forms taught are after *like* and *go*, not a tense), **no present perfect, no comparatives/superlatives, no possessive *'s*, no *must/should***.

## Grade 3 — English for Kazakhstan Grade 3 Pupil's Book — ⚠ TITLE MISMATCH vs the record: the book never calls itself "Give Me Five!"

Record title it was filed under: "Give Me Five! Grade 3 Pupil's Book", Ramsden/Shaw/Tokzhanova, Macmillan, 2024. **Verdict: the file is the right book but the printed title is different.** The title printed on the cover, title page and back cover is *English for Kazakhstan Grade 3 — Pupil's Book*. "Give Me Five!" is the name of the Macmillan parent series this Kazakhstan edition adapts (the back-cover reference list and the course design are Macmillan's, and the record's author/publisher data otherwise fit); the words "Give Me Five!" do not appear on the pages examined. Tokzhanova is credited as "Teacher consultant", not as an author.

### Identity block

- **Real title as printed (title page, PDF p. 1 = printed p. 1):** *ENGLISH for Kazakhstan, Grade 3 — PUPIL'S BOOK*
- **Authors as printed (title page):** Donna Shaw • Joanne Ramsden; **Teacher consultant Aigul Tokzhanova** (record order "Ramsden/Shaw" reversed vs the book; Tokzhanova is consultant, not co-author)
- **Publisher:** Macmillan Education (publisher's logo on the back cover, PDF p. 129; the Reference page, printed p. 128, lists the Macmillan sources the course draws on)
- **Edition / year as printed:** **No publication year and no ISBN are printed on the title page, syllabus pages, Reference page or back cover of this file** — the file contains no imprint page. The record's "2024" could not be confirmed from the book itself and is not promoted here.
- **ISBN:** None printed anywhere in the pages examined (cover, title, syllabus, dictionary end, reference page, back cover).
- **PDF pages:** 129
- **Printed page numbers / offset:** **printed page = PDF page** (offset 0) throughout: the Syllabus carries the folio "2 two" on PDF p. 2 and "4 four" on PDF p. 4, the Dictionary ends on printed p. 127 = PDF p. 127, and the Reference page is printed p. 128 = PDF p. 128; PDF p. 129 is the unnumbered back cover. Folios are printed both as numerals and as words ("one hundred and twenty-seven"). All page references below are to the printed pages.
- **Text layer:** Unusable — this PDF has no usable embedded text at all (129 bytes of text layer in total); the book is page images, read from page renders with OCR of the renders as a search index. Citation anchors were verified against the OCR/render of the exact page cited.
- **How the book is built:** A "Learn the alphabet" opener (p. 5), then eight units of 14 pages each (pp. 6–117) and a Dictionary (pp. 118–127) with IPA transcriptions and Kazakh and Russian equivalents, plus a Reference page (p. 128). The two-page Syllabus (pp. 2–3) maps every unit across six strands: Vocabulary, Grammar, Phonics, Literacy, Culture (each unit pairs Kazakhstan with Britain) and 21st Century Skills. Every unit carries a literacy text type (song, poster, rhyme, postcard, birthday card, poem, play script, diary).

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Learn the alphabet (p. 5).** A one-page alphabet presentation with the alphabet song (the unit's Literacy text), before Unit 1 begins.

**Unit 1 — Hello, English! (pp. 6–19).** Vocabulary: greetings and names, colours, countries and everyday objects. Grammar as mapped in the Syllabus: *What's your name? — I'm …; What colour is it? — It's …; What's this? — It's a …; How old are you? — I'm …; Where are you from? — I'm from …; How do you spell?*; numbers 1–100; subject and object pronouns. Phonics: the sounds of the alphabet. Culture: names, surnames and cities in Kazakhstan and Britain. Skill: ordering favourite things.

**Unit 2 — My school (pp. 20–33).** Vocabulary: objects in a schoolbag, classroom objects and classroom instructions. Grammar: *where* questions with *to be*, numbers 1–100, countable and uncountable nouns, the imperative, *how many*, prepositions of place, *this/these – that/those*, *there is/are*, *have/has got*, and regular and irregular plural nouns. Phonics: c [k], ch [tʃ], sh [ʃ], r [r]. Literacy: a classroom poster. Culture: school clubs in Kazakhstan and Britain. Skill: being organised.

**Unit 3 — People I love (pp. 34–47).** Vocabulary: park objects, park activities, family members and descriptive adjectives. Grammar: *there is/are*, *Where's …? / Where are …?*, subject and object pronouns, prepositions of place, *have/has got* and the present simple (first presentation). Phonics: th [ð], w [w] and plural noun endings. Literacy: an action rhyme. Culture: parks in Kazakhstan and Britain. Skill: being careful.

**Unit 4 — Weather (pp. 48–61).** Vocabulary: seasons and seasonal activities, weather, clothes and descriptive adjectives. Grammar: the present simple continued, prepositions of time, subject and object pronouns and the articles. Phonics: ea [iː], ee [iː], v [v], ir [ɜː], or [ɔː] and the verb ending *-(e)s*. Literacy: a postcard. Culture: seasonal traditions in Kazakhstan and Britain. Skill: making choices.

**Unit 5 — My free time (pp. 62–75).** Vocabulary: sports, actions, the days of the week, descriptive adjectives and toys. Grammar: the present simple, *go/like + verb + -ing*, *play + noun*, *let's*, the imperative, *can/can't* and the articles. Phonics: ng [ŋ], ai [aɪ], ay [eɪ], th [θ]. Literacy: a birthday card. Culture: birthday parties in Kazakhstan and Britain. Skill: joining in.

**Unit 6 — Health (pp. 76–89).** Vocabulary: body parts, descriptive adjectives, food items, games and sport. Grammar: *have/has got*, singular and plural nouns, *this/that/these/those*, subject and object pronouns, the present simple and *there is/are*. Phonics: o [əʊ], i [aɪ], a [eɪ], a [æ], h [h]. Literacy: a poem. Culture: traditional sports in Kazakhstan and Britain. Skill: eating healthily.

**Unit 7 — Buildings (pp. 90–103).** Vocabulary: rooms, furniture and buildings in a town (the Dictionary's Unit 7 set runs to town words such as museum, police station, post office, train station, tower, and story words like haunted house, ghost, magic, prince, pumpkin, tale). Grammar: *there is/are*, prepositions of place, *How many …?* with numbers 1–100, *have/has got*, *this/that/these/those*, singular and plural nouns and *here/there*. Phonics: oo [uː], ar [ɑː], ow [aʊ], ou [aʊ]. Literacy: a play script. Culture: famous buildings in Kazakhstan and Britain. Skill (digital): helping at home. Directions language appears in the Dictionary here: *Go straight on. Turn left. Turn right.*

**Unit 8 — My holidays (pp. 104–117).** Vocabulary: holiday places and objects, transport and descriptive adjectives — the Dictionary's Unit 8 set includes aquarium, beach, boat, bus, castle, chocolate factory, circus, funfair, helicopter, lorry, motorbike, safari park, science museum, sports centre, theatre, train, water park, plus *Here's my ticket*, *Let's go to the circus!* and *Have a nice holiday!* Grammar: *let's*, *How many …?*, prepositions of place and time, interrogative pronouns and *can/can't*, *here/there*. Phonics: wh [w], wh [h], z [z]. Literacy: a diary. Culture: holiday places in Kazakhstan. Skill: using a digital camera.

**Dictionary (pp. 118–127).** The course vocabulary by unit, each entry with IPA transcription and Kazakh and Russian equivalents; the Reference page (p. 128) lists the Macmillan dictionaries, grammar and readers series used.

**Grammar spine:** *To be* questions (*what's your name, how old, where … from*); subject and object pronouns; *this/these – that/those*; *there is/are* and *where's/where are*; *have/has got*; plurals (regular and irregular) and countable/uncountable nouns; *how many* with numbers to 100; prepositions of place and time; the imperative and *let's*; the articles; the present simple (Units 3–6); *can/can't*; *like/go + -ing* and *play + noun*; interrogative pronouns. A full phonics strand (letter sounds, digraphs and vowel patterns, unit by unit) runs alongside.

### Honest negatives — what this book does not teach

- **No past tenses and no future forms** anywhere in the Syllabus grammar map: no past simple, no *was/were*, no *will/going to*. Narrative exists only in the literacy texts (rhyme, play script, diary), whose language is not presented as a tense point.
- **No present continuous as a tense** (the *-ing* taught is after *like/go*), **no present perfect, no comparatives/superlatives, no possessive *'s*, no *must/should*, no *some/any* system** (countable/uncountable is taught without the some/any pair in the map).
- **No telling the time** (the days of the week appear in Unit 5; months are absent — seasons carry the calendar load), and numbers are capped at 100.
- Identity data is thin in this file: with no imprint page, the book as held cannot evidence its own year or ISBN — both must come from another copy before the record's "Macmillan, 2024" is treated as verified.

## Grade 3 — Smiles for Kazakhstan (Grade 3) Pupil's Book — ⚠ YEAR MISMATCH: book prints 2018, record says 2017; the file is the print file and has no covers

Record: "Smiles for Kazakhstan (Grade 3) Pupil's Book", Evans & Dooley, Express Publishing, 2017, 128 pp. **Verdict: same book, wrong year and wrong lead author credit.** The title page (PDF p. 1) credits Jenny Dooley alone as author (Bob Obee is Series Consultant, N. Mukhamedjanova is translator), and its Kazakh imprint line prints **2018**, not 2017. The PDF is the publisher's print file — every page carries crop marks and a Quark production slug (module files dated 5/16/18; the Glossary file is dated 20/05/2019) — and it contains no front or back cover at all, so no ISBN is present in the file.

### Identity block

- **Real title as printed (title page, PDF p. 1):** *Smiles — Pupil's Book for Kazakhstan Grade 3*
- **Author as printed:** Jenny Dooley; **Series Consultant: Bob Obee; Translations by N. Mukhamedjanova** (the record's "Evans & Dooley" does not match this book: Virginia Evans is not credited in it)
- **Publisher:** Express Publishing
- **Edition / year as printed:** Kazakh imprint line on the title page «Экспресс Паблишин баспасы 2018» — printed year **2018** (record says 2017 — mismatch). Production slugs inside the file are dated May 2018 (modules) and 20 May 2019 (Glossary), so the held print file was still being revised in 2019.
- **ISBN:** Not present — the file has no covers (it begins at the title page and ends at the last Glossary page), and no ISBN is printed on the title page.
- **PDF pages:** 128
- **Printed page numbers / offset:** **printed page = PDF page** (offset 0): PDF p. 1 = title page, PDF pp. 2–3 = Contents, Module 1 opens on printed p. 4 = PDF p. 4, and the Glossary runs printed pp. 124–128 = PDF pp. 124–128. All page references below are to the printed pages.
- **Text layer:** Unusable — the PDF's only embedded text is the okulyk watermark overlay; the book is page images, read from page renders with OCR of the renders as a search index. Citation anchors were verified against the OCR/render of the exact page cited.
- **How the book is built:** Eight 14-page modules in the Smiles pattern (photo opener, presentation, cartoon/reading spreads, cross-curricular "Thinking Cap" tasks, project), four two-page Storytime comic stories with a "Smiles Values!" moral, and a trilingual Glossary (pp. 124–128) listing every module's vocabulary with Kazakh and Russian equivalents (no IPA in this book). Contents are on printed pp. 2–3.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Module 1 — Animals (pp. 4–17).** A photo opener (elephant, snake, penguin, p. 4) and "Animal Types" (p. 5): classifying animals as mammal, reptile or bird, solving present-simple riddles ("I live on land. I eat plants. What am I? I'm a(n) …"), and a true/false quiz, "A Snake's Life", that practises *Do snakes live in Antarctica? — Yes, they do / No, they don't.* The Glossary's Module 1 set is the full menagerie (butterfly, cat, chicken, cow, dog, duck, eagle, elephant, frog, giant pandas, giraffes, goat, horse, parrot, penguin, pony, rabbit, sea lions, snake, spider, white lions, zebras) plus animal body parts (ears, eyes, hair, hands, head, legs, mouth, nose).

**Storytime I — Twinklinka (pp. 32–33, told after Module 2).** "A story from Sri Lanka": a ten-frame comic in present-tense dialogue about a beautiful doll who is bought, dirtied, rejected as "dirty and ugly", and finally taken home by a poor girl — "You aren't ugly now. You are so beautiful! … You've got a heart of gold!" Smiles Values: *Be kind to everyone!* Story vocabulary: princess, beautiful, money, dirty, ugly.

**Module 2 — Light and Dark (pp. 18–31).** Road safety and being seen: the Glossary set is reflective clothing and night vocabulary (belts, boots, candle, cap, gloves, helmet, jeans, jumper, lamp, light, reflective, scarf, stars, torch, traffic lights, uniform, vests, zips; put on / take off) with behaviour phrases (be quiet, chase, chew gum, dish, drop litter, make noise, shout).

**Module 3 — Time (pp. 34–47).** "Gulnara's Daily Life" (p. 34): a wheel of one Kazakh girl's day — get up, have breakfast, go to school, come home from school, do my homework, have dinner, watch TV, go to bed, brush my teeth, have a shower. Page 35 adds the ordinal numbers first–tenth through coloured alarm clocks ("The first and the ninth clocks are blue") and a preposition review (*in front of, between, on, in, under* — "Where's the laptop? — It's in the schoolbag"). The module teaches the days of the week, numbers to a hundred (twenty … ninety) and telling the time, including the Glossary's phrases *It's half past one*, *It's quarter past one* and *It's quarter to one*.

**Module 4 — Buildings (pp. 48–61).** Homes and the town: rooms and furniture (armchair, bath, bathroom, bedroom, bookcase, cooker, fridge, garage, garden, hall, house, kitchen, living room, mirror, sofa) and the town set with its people (bank, hospital, post office, supermarket, baker's, butcher's, greengrocer's, sweet shop; baker, chef, doctor, firefighter, lollipop lady, pilot, police officer, postman, vet) — the Glossary's Module 4 list oddly opens with "armchair, astronaut" before this set, as printed.

**Storytime II — The Lazy Girl (pp. 62–63).** Story vocabulary: housework, hungry, lazy, water, well.

**Module 5 — Art and Music (pp. 64–77).** Leisure, music and sport: pop music, rock music, classical music, hip hop; play the violin, pluck (a guitar), read comics, paint, plant flowers; sports (badminton, baseball, ice hockey, table tennis, volleyball), go shopping, go to the gym, plus lie, pie, policeman, shake, strike, tie and clean in the Glossary set.

**Module 6 — Explorers and Inventors (pp. 78–91).** The twelve months (January–December, all in the Glossary) and the explorer's world: bird, cave, diver, footprint, metal, plastic, rock, vine, wood, scary, scream.

**Storytime III — Jack and the Giant (pp. 92–93).** Story vocabulary: angry, bowl, giant, porridge, taste.

**Module 7 — Water, Water Everywhere! (pp. 94–107).** The water cycle in science words (cloud formation, condense, evaporation, precipitation, water vapour) and water sports and actions (dive, get wet, have a picnic, hop, jog, sail, skateboard, skip, surf, waterski, do karate, play the violin, pool, walrus, whiskers).

**Module 8 — Having Fun (pp. 108–121).** Seasons and free time: the four seasons (autumn, spring, summer, winter) with an activity set in the present simple (drink milk, eat an ice cream, fish, fly a kite, go to school, listen to music, pick flowers, ride a bike, sleep, tidy my room, watch TV) and places (cinema, market, skating rink, sports centre, sheep).

**Storytime IV — Pennies from the Sky (pp. 122–123).** Story vocabulary: kind, pennies, poor, sky.

**Glossary (pp. 124–128).** Every module's and story's vocabulary in English–Kazakh–Russian columns (no phonetic transcriptions in this book).

**Grammar spine:** Judged from the module presentations and the Glossary's phrase entries: the present simple in statements, questions and short answers (*Do snakes live in Antarctica? — Yes, they do / No, they don't*, from Module 1); *have got* (*You've got a heart of gold*); prepositions of place (*in front of, between, on, in, under*) with *Where's the …?*; daily-routine phrases in the present simple; ordinal numbers first–tenth; numbers to 100; days and months; telling the time (o'clock, half past, quarter past/to). No grammar is tabulated anywhere; structures ride inside the activities.

### Honest negatives — what this book does not teach

- **No past tenses and no future forms are presented**: even the four stories are told in present-tense speech bubbles, so there is no past-simple narration anywhere in the book; no *will/going to*, no present perfect, no conditionals, no passive.

## Grade 4 — Smiles for Kazakhstan Grade 4 Pupil's Book

### Identity block

- **Real title as printed (title page, PDF p. 1):** *Smiles — Pupil's Book for Kazakhstan Grade 4*
- **Author as printed:** Jenny Dooley; **Series Consultant: Bob Obee; Translations by N. Mukhamedjanova** (the record's "Dooley & Evans" does not match: Virginia Evans is not credited in this book)
- **Publisher:** Express Publishing
- **Edition / year as printed:** The record gives no year; the title page's Kazakh imprint line prints **2019** («Экспресс Паблишин баспасы 2019»). The PDF is again the publisher's print file: production slugs are dated 04/12/2018 (modules and Contents), 02/05/2019 (Modules 2 and 4) and 5/27/19 (Glossary).
- **ISBN:** Not present — the file has no covers; it begins at the title page and ends at the last Glossary page (printed p. 144).
- **PDF pages:** 144
- **Printed page numbers / offset:** **printed page = PDF page** (offset 0) throughout (Contents printed pp. 2–3 = PDF pp. 2–3; Module 1 opens printed p. 4; Glossary printed pp. 132–144). All page references below are to the printed pages.
- **Identity verdict:** The file is the book the record intends (Smiles Grade 4 pupil's book, Express Publishing), with two credit corrections: the author as printed is Jenny Dooley alone (Obee as series consultant), and the printed year is 2019.
- **Text layer:** Unusable — the PDF's only embedded text is the okulyk watermark overlay; the book is page images, read from page renders with OCR of the renders as a search index. Citation anchors were verified against the OCR/render of the exact page cited.
- **How the book is built:** Eight modules mapped in a full Contents syllabus (printed pp. 2–3: Vocabulary, Grammar, Skills, Thinking Skills, Cross-Curricular Links/Culture and Phonics/Word-Building for every module), four "Presentation Skills" project spreads, four Storytime stories with stated values, and a trilingual Glossary (pp. 132–144, no IPA). Each module has two sub-units per theme and a named writing product (email, blog entry, postcard, biography).

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Module 1 — Kazakhstan in the World of Sport (pp. 4–17).** Sports and children's games: team vs individual sports (bandy, football, ice hockey, cross-country skiing, tennis; equipment odd-one-out, p. 5), playground games (play hopscotch, hide-and-seek, leap frog, tag, blind man's buff; have a sack race), Aesop's fables and Olympic Games spreads. Pair talk recycles frequency: "I sometimes play hopscotch with my friends." Fact box (p. 5): "1951 was the year when the Medeu Sports Centre near Almaty opened. It is the highest ice skating rink in the world!" Grammar (Contents): present simple, adverbs of time/frequency/manner, time clauses, interrogative pronouns, the imperative, possessive adjectives and comparisons. Writing: an email inviting a friend to a sports event. Phonics: j–g (jellyfish, jewellery, giant, gem).

**Module 2 — Values in Myths and Legends (pp. 18–31).** Traditional stories, people and places, dragons and creatures; the reading text is The Legend of Korkyt Ata, and the opener's "Did you know?" points to Copenhagen's Little Mermaid statue (p. 18). Grammar: **the past simple is taught here for the first time in the series**, alongside the infinitive, *-ing* forms, exclamatory sentences, comparisons, plurals and the possessive case; the writing task is "a short text about when you were little". **Presentation Skills I: Mythical Creatures (pp. 32–33).**

**Module 3 — Treasure and Heritage (pp. 36–49).** Treasure maps, treasure and numbers, and "Our Planet's Treasure": cardinal numbers 1–1,000, ordinal numbers 1–100 and natural features. Grammar: plurals, the possessive case, determiners, the imperative, *have got*, *there is/are*, prepositions, articles, interrogative pronouns and the present simple. Writing: an email about your last holidays. **Storytime I (pp. 34–35): The Legend of William Tell** — value: "Love gives people strength and courage."

**Module 4 — Professions and Ways of Communication (pp. 50–63).** Jobs, means of transport and communicating around the world, including a body-language spread and a technology spread; the opener's "Did you know?" shows one of the first TVs, "about 70 years ago" (p. 50). Grammar: plurals, possessive case and adjectives, *this/that/these/those*, object pronouns, comparisons, *have got*, *there is/are*. Expressions with *get* are the Word Gym slot. Writing: a short text about a community helper. **Presentation Skills II: A Popular Holiday Destination (pp. 64–65). Storytime II (pp. 66–67): The Trumpeter of Kraków** — value: "Showing respect."

**Module 5 — Hot and Cold (pp. 68–81).** Weather 1 and 2, volcanoes, snow and ice: the weather phrases (*It's snowing / cloudy / sunny / windy / foggy / hot / cold / raining*, p. 69), summer and winter activities, and a miming game that introduces the **present continuous** — "What am I doing? — You're swimming. It's summer!" Grammar: the present continuous and *-ing* forms, *have to / must / mustn't* (obligation), *can* (request/permission), *have + object + infinitive*, prepositions of place and time; camping and extreme-weather vocabulary (the Glossary adds avalanche, campfire, first-aid kit, hail, tornado, thunder and lightning). Writing: a text about a campsite.

**Module 6 — Healthy World (pp. 82–95).** Food and drinks, healthy bodies, saving animals and helping the planet: strange snacks from around the world, junk food vs healthy food, and container phrases (a bar of chocolate, a bottle of cola, a carton of juice, a jar of honey, a loaf of bread, a packet of peas, a pot of yogurt). Grammar: present simple and **past simple** together, adverbs, possessive adjectives/case, comparisons, the present continuous (present and future meaning), *some/any*, *-ing* forms, plurals, interrogative pronouns and the imperative. Writing: a blog entry about healthy food. **Presentation Skills III: Endangered Animals (pp. 96–97)** — aggressive, become extinct, chop off, gentle, leader, male, tusk, vegetarian, well-hidden. **Storytime III (pp. 98–99): The Town with Lazy People** — value: "Hard work is rewarded."

**Module 7 — Journey into Space (pp. 100–113).** Into space, planets and aliens, mixed with holiday language (buy souvenirs, cash, cheque in the Glossary): space words and the planets, with CLIL on Mars, the Baikonur Cosmodrome Museum in Kazakhstan and the National Space Centre in the UK; the speaking syllabus is holiday plans and making predictions. Grammar: the full recycling set — possessive adjectives/case, comparisons, interrogative pronouns, *can*, *must/mustn't/have to*, *have + object + infinitive*, plurals, *this/that/these/those*, articles, present simple, past simple and the present continuous (present and future meaning). Writing: a holiday postcard about your dream holiday.

**Module 8 — Machines (pp. 114–127).** Slow machines, fast machines and robots; cool gadgets. Materials and machines vocabulary (calculate, cassette player, digital thermometer, dishwasher, fridge, hairdryer, hoverboard, kettle, microwave, remote control, touchscreen, typewriter, vacuum cleaner, washing machine; It's made of …, It's used for …). Grammar: past simple, interrogative pronouns, adverbs of time/frequency and manner, possessive adjectives, *this/that/these/those*, object pronouns, comparisons, prepositions, *can/must/mustn't/have to*, articles, *have + object + infinitive*, determiners, adjectives, *have got*, *there is/are*, the infinitive and *-ing* forms. Writing: a biography of a person in history. **Presentation Skills IV: Technology (pp. 128–129). Storytime IV (pp. 130–131): Daedalus and Icarus** — value: "Don't be overconfident" (the Storytime IV Glossary set is the myth's: maze, minotaur, wings, escape, prisoner, heartbroken, overconfident).

**Glossary (pp. 132–144).** All module, Presentation Skills and Storytime vocabulary in English–Kazakh–Russian columns (no phonetic transcriptions).

**Grammar spine:** The present simple and the **past simple** (taught from Module 2 and recycled thereafter); the **present continuous** (Module 5), also used with future meaning (Modules 6–7); comparisons (comparative adjectives, from Module 1); possessive adjectives and the possessive case; *have got*; *there is/are*; *this/that/these/those* and object pronouns; plurals and determiners/articles; interrogative pronouns; the imperative; *can* (request/permission); *must/mustn't/have to* (obligation); *have + object + infinitive*; the infinitive and *-ing* forms; *some/any*; adverbs of time, frequency and manner; exclamatory sentences; numbers to 1,000 and ordinals to 100.

### Honest negatives — what this book does not teach

- **No present perfect, no passive voice, no conditionals and no reported speech** anywhere in the Contents grammar map.

## Grade 4 — English Language for Kazakhstan Grade 4 Pupil's Book

### Identity block

- **Real title as printed (front cover, PDF p. 1; title page, PDF p. 3):** *English Language for Kazakhstan Grade 4* (cover: "for Kazakhstan Grade 4", with DIGI APP)
- **Authors as printed (cover and title page):** Jenny Dooley – Bob Obee; **Series Consultant: Natalya Mukhamedjanova** (the record lists her as third author; the book credits her as series consultant, as in the Grade 3 volume)
- **Publisher:** Express Publishing (imprint, PDF p. 2; the Grade 3 volume's imprint thanks LLP "EDU Stream" for photography — the record's publisher line is "EDU Stream", the book's imprint is Express Publishing)
- **Edition / year as printed:** First published **2025** (imprint, PDF p. 2). Matches the record's 2025.
- **ISBN as printed (back cover, PDF p. 116):** ISBN 978-1-3992-1420-9
- **PDF pages:** 116
- **Printed page numbers / offset:** **printed page = PDF page − 2** throughout (Contents printed pp. 4–5 = PDF pp. 6–7; Module 1 opens printed p. 6 = PDF p. 8; Glossary ends printed p. 112 = PDF p. 114; PDF p. 115 is the unnumbered Alphabet poster, PDF p. 116 the back cover). All page references below are to the **printed** pages.
- **Identity verdict:** The file matches the record (Grade 4, Express Publishing, 2025), with the same credit nuance as the Grade 3 volume: Mukhamedjanova is series consultant, not co-author.
- **Text layer:** Unusable — the PDF's only embedded text is the okulyk watermark overlay; the book is page images, read from page renders with OCR of the renders as a search index. Citation anchors were verified against the OCR/render of the exact page cited.
- **How the book is built:** The direct sequel to the Grade 3 Express volume (record #1619): eight 12-page modules in two sub-units each, a "Sounds and Words" phonics/sight-words strand, a CLIL page and a named writing task per module (Contents syllabus, printed pp. 4–5), "A World of Wonder" spreads after Modules 4 and 8 (pp. 54–55, 104–105), two Storytime fables with values (pp. 106–107), a trilingual Glossary with IPA (pp. 108–112) and the Alphabet poster (PDF p. 115).

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Module 1 — All about me (pp. 6–17).** Back to school; my daily routine. Vocabulary: school subjects (Art, English, Geography, History, ICT, Maths, Music, PE, Science), daily routines (do my homework, get up, go to bed, go to school, have breakfast, have dinner, have lunch, watch TV), the days of the week and cardinal numbers 1–1,000. Grammar: *like + verb-ing*, the present simple, adverbs and prepositions of time, ordinal numbers, adverbs of frequency and *can*. CLIL (Maths): solving maths puzzles. Reading: "Daily routines"; writing: Back to school! Sounds: revision of letters and sounds; sight words *like, make, look, play, go, too*.

**Module 2 — My World (pp. 18–29).** My family and friends; learn from the good, hate the bad. Vocabulary: the wider family (aunt, cousin, grandparents, parents, uncle), chores (do the dishes, do the shopping, feed the fish, make the beds, take out the rubbish, tidy the room, walk the dog, water the plants) and rules (be quiet, chew gum, drop litter, make noise, shout, wear a uniform). Grammar: the possessive case, the verb *to be*, singular and plural nouns, the modal verbs *can* and *must*, the articles and the present simple. CLIL (Social Science): what babies can/can't do. Reading: "Doing chores"; writing: My Caring Family! Sounds: consonant blends (bl, br, cl, gr, pl); sight words *this, these, under, floor, two, let's*.

**Module 3 — My Hobby (pp. 30–41).** Sports and exercises; arts and music. Vocabulary: sports (badminton, baseball, basketball, hockey, rugby, table tennis), games (have a sack race, jog, play a board game, play chess, play hide-and-seek, play hopscotch, play tag, skip) and musical instruments, Kazakh ones included (dombyra, kobyz, zhetygen alongside drum, flute, piano, tambourine, violin). Grammar: subject and object pronouns, possessive pronouns, the **present continuous**, the present simple, the imperative, *let's + verb* and the infinitive of purpose. CLIL (PE): rules of sport. Reading: "Sports Day"; writing: My Favourite Sport. Sounds: a_e (same, game, cake, snake) and i_e (time, bike, nine, kite); sight words *say, read, sing, game, well, best*.

**Module 4 — People and Jobs (pp. 42–53).** Professions; what do you do? Vocabulary: professions (baker, doctor, farmer, firefighter, greengrocer, police officer, postman, vet), the items of each job (bag, coat, helmet, tractor, uniform, van) and job actions (bring letters and parcels, fight fires, grow fruit, help sick people, make bread, sell vegetables). Grammar: the possessive case, *to be*, the articles, the present simple, subject and object pronouns, possessive pronouns and adjectives and question words. CLIL (Geography): be safe in the street! Reading: "Chico running away"; writing: I am a community helper! Sounds: i_e and *igh* (night, lighter); sight words *try, light, kind, right, bye, fire*. **A World of Wonder 1 (pp. 54–55)** recycles Modules 1–4.

**Module 5 — Food and Drinks (pp. 56–67).** Traditional food; daily meals. Vocabulary: a wide food set (beef, biscuit, bread, butter, cereal, cocoa, cola, corn, crisps, fish, lemon, lettuce, lollipop, milk, noodles, olive, pasta, peas, pizza, sausages, steak, sugar, tuna, vinegar, yogurt). Grammar: **comparative adjectives** (first presentation), *this/these – that/those*, *have got*, the imperative, *there is – there are*, the present simple and adverbs of manner. CLIL (Design and Technology): are you a healthy eater? Reading: "Chico eating the boys' popcorn"; writing: Funny Food. Sounds: *ch* (cheek, touch, chocolate, cheese, cherries, chicken, chips); sight words *call, chair, cold, chicken, cut, children*.

**Module 6 — Animals (pp. 68–79).** On the farm; wild animals and the places they live. Vocabulary: animals (camel, chicken, cow, crocodile, dolphin, duck, elephant, horse, goat, gorilla, lizard, penguin, polar bear, sheep, tiger, whale), parts of animals' bodies (body, ears, eyes, head, legs, mouth, nose, tail, teeth) and habitats (jungle, ocean, polar region, steppe). Grammar: the articles, plurals, *this/these – that/those*, the **past simple of the verb *to be* (*was/were*)** — the only past-tense point in the book — prepositions of place and the imperative; the speaking syllabus is "asking and answering questions about the past". CLIL (Science): animals that have/haven't got skeletons. Reading: "A day at the zoo"; writing: My Favourite Animal! Sounds: *sh* (ship, fish, shell); sight words *walk, your, how, fast, they, many*.

**Module 7 — Travelling (pp. 80–91).** Places around us; Kazakhstan and Great Britain. Vocabulary: natural features (forest, island, lake, mountain, river), town buildings (bank, butcher's, hotel, library, museum, sports centre) and the UK set (England, Great Britain, Northern Ireland, Scotland, Wales beside Kazakhstan). Grammar: the past simple of *to be* again, prepositions of place, plurals, the articles, the imperative and *this/these – that/those*; speaking covers giving and following directions and talking about one's city ("asking about where people were at the weekend" in the listening syllabus). CLIL (Geography): how to use a map! Reading: "A fun day"; writing: Sweet memories. Sounds: *ow* (town, clown, cow) and *ou* (house, around, mouse, mouth); sight words *so, ride, up, see, street, by*.

**Module 8 — Wonderful World (pp. 92–103).** Inventions and bright ideas; famous people. Vocabulary: gadgets and inventions (digital camera, games console, GPS, headphones, laptop, remote control, smartphone, smartwatch; battery and electricity in the Glossary) and jobs (actor, astronaut, athlete, painter, singer, writer). Grammar: comparatives and **superlatives**, question words, subject and object pronouns, *this/these – that/those*, the imperative, *must* and prepositions of direction. CLIL (ICT): computing. Reading: "A bright idea"; writing: A fun invention. Sounds: *ee* (queen, bee, week, seeds) and *ea* (leave, leaf, season); sight words *sleep, keep, feet, sheep, street, tree*. **A World of Wonder 2 (pp. 104–105)** recycles Modules 5–8 (badger, beak, bear, eagle, feather, monkey, strawberry).

**Storytime (pp. 106–107).** "The Cuckoo" (value: There is good in everyone!) and "The Dancing Tortoise" (value: Be clever and brave!) — the Glossary gives their story words (cuckoo, forest, mole, seed; cage, thirsty, tortoise).

**Glossary (pp. 108–112).** Module wordlists in English with IPA and Kazakh and Russian equivalents, including the World of Wonder and Storytime sets.

**Grammar spine:** The present simple (with adverbs of frequency and time); *like + -ing*; the present continuous (Module 3); comparatives (Module 5) and superlatives (Module 8); the possessive case and possessive pronouns/adjectives; *have got*; *there is/are*; *this/these – that/those*; articles; plurals; *can* and *must*; question words; the imperative and *let's*; the infinitive of purpose; and, as the book's only past-tense teaching, the **past simple of *to be* (*was/were*)** in Modules 6–7, used to talk about animals, places and the weekend.

### Honest negatives — what this book does not teach

- **No past simple of ordinary verbs**: *was/were* is the whole past-tense syllabus — there are no regular or irregular lexical past forms, no past questions with *did*, and no past narration teaching (the Storytime fables carry their stories without a past-tense grammar point).
- **No future forms** (*will/going to* absent), **no present perfect, no passive, no conditionals, no reported speech**.
- **No *have to / should / might*** (modality is *can* and *must* only), **no *some/any* point** in the Contents map, and no telling-the-time revision beyond routine times.
- Numbers stop at 1,000 (cardinals) with ordinals; there is no money, measurement or date system beyond days of the week.
